# Status

This page reads `main`, not a release: it gives the measured state of
podssh at the newest commit. Each result has its date and the command that
measured it.

## Where things stand

Read on 2026-11-24; the rows below are the measurements.

- **Releases.** The newest is `v1.1.0-beta.3` (2026-11-05); the newest stable is `v1.0.1`
  (2026-11-03). `v1.1.0`, the release of M10, waits for the move of the shared crates to
  PodUtils/podcrates (T-684, T-446); betas may go out before it.
- **What works.** The SSH client with the options of OpenSSH, the files commands (`cp`, `mv`,
  `scp`, `sftp`, `edit`), `proxy` and `pipe`, the reverse road (`node`, `operator`, `relay`) with
  the resumable layer, `podssh relay serve` (the relay as a role of podssh), the net of a user's own
  nodes (`net`, exit, routes, `share`, `funnel`), `doctor`, `keygen`, `man`, `chat`, `mcp` and
  `update`; each with its rows below. `podssh serve --stdio` is in work, milestone M5 (T-107 to T-114).
- **The roads.** Beside the relay: `--direct`, iroh, WARP, Tor, Tailscale's DERP, the HTTP and
  HTTP/2 roads in part (T-319, T-321), the quick tunnel, and the public carriers and last fallbacks of M10 (Piping Server,
  Wisp, OpenTunnel, croc, Serveo, ssh-j, localhost.run, localtunnel, bore and others), and ZeroTier's
  road, live through ZeroTier Inc.'s roots and relay and in the release's features on Unix (T-733).
- **The gate.** Green at `dd1f9a9` on `main` (2026-11-24, run 45413276289, each job).
- **Where the work is:** [TODO/PROGRESS.md](../TODO/PROGRESS.md) (the queue and the order) and
  [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md) (the next step of each partial entry).

The newest release is `v1.1.0-beta.3` (2026-11-05, at `6d30328`), a beta;
the newest that is not a beta is `v1.0.1` (2026-11-03, at `6ece1f9`). A row
of work done after `v1.0.0` (at `35a0d19`) names in its first cell the first
release that holds it, or says `unreleased`: no release holds it, and a
binary of a release lacks it. A row with neither is in `v1.0.0` and in each
release after it. A sentence inside a row has its own date, and one dated
after a release can tell of work that the release lacks. `podssh --version`
names the version of a binary, and the commit of a release's binary, and
`podssh update --check` the release that an update would take. The release binaries are built with
the features `iroh` and `warp`, not `ts` or `tor`
([development.md](development.md), section "Release builds").
`scripts/check-repo.py` holds the marks: it fails a row dated 2026-11-02 or
later with neither, and a row that says `unreleased` with a date before the
newest release's.

When the state changes, update this page in the same commit as the change.
A new row of work says `unreleased`; the commit after a tag names the tag in
each such row that the tag holds.

Measured from 2026-10-08: a Windows 11 host with native `cargo 1.98.0` and
`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4`, the `rust:1-alpine` container gate, CI, a Podman box,
and two Linux hosts on a tailnet; from 2026-10-30, also a Claude Code cloud
container (Linux x86_64, Rust 1.99.0) with its own Docker and Podman. Each
row has its own date.

## Summary

These commands work: `podssh ssh`, `podssh each`, `podssh run`, `podssh job`,
`podssh session`, `podssh containers`, `podssh proxy`, `podssh pipe`,
`podssh funnel`, `podssh cp`, `podssh mv`, `podssh scp`,
`podssh sftp` and `podssh edit` (files), `podssh doctor`, `podssh keygen`,
`podssh agent`, `podssh man`, `podssh status`, `podssh ping`,
`podssh health`, `podssh node`, `podssh operator`, `podssh relay`,
`podssh chat`, `podssh play`, `podssh detached`, `podssh config`,
`podssh mcp`, `podssh net` and `podssh update`. `podssh ts`, the iroh road and
WARP's `warp://` refuse with exit code 70 in a build without their features.

- `podssh ssh` is an SSH client on `russh`, with the command line of
  OpenSSH. It passes the 301 interop checks of the gate against OpenSSH and
  Dropbear servers and stand-in relays, each scored against its line of
  `scripts/interop-baseline.txt`, 14 of 14 interactive checks in a Windows
  console, and real logins through the live relay.
- `podssh proxy HOST PORT` carries a TCP stream through the relay, directly
  or through an HTTP CONNECT or a SOCKS5 proxy. It works as the
  `ProxyCommand` of OpenSSH.
- `podssh doctor` tells what a host allows and whether the path works.
- `podssh keygen` makes keys that OpenSSH reads and accepts.
- `podssh man` is the whole manual, generated by the binary from its own
  tables. It needs nothing but the binary.
- podssh fails over between relay hosts, and finds a silent relay in 30 to
  40 s. It works with no DNS.
- podssh was measured in two real sandboxes on 2026-10-08, and in a Claude
  Code cloud container on 2026-10-30 (below). M3 is done (2026-10-09).
  Betas `v1.0.0-beta.N` come before `v1.0.0`, the final release (T-309,
  T-250): `v1.0.0-beta.1` is published (2026-10-30, at `5c8d423`), and
  `v1.0.0-beta.2` (2026-10-31, at `f7f97a5`) and `v1.0.0-beta.3`
  (2026-11-01, at `54d89a0`), each with the provenance of each binary and a
  signature of its checksums. `v1.0.0` is published (2026-11-02, at
  `35a0d19`), and `v1.0.1` with the repairs of its testers' defects
  (2026-11-03, at `6ece1f9`; `release-proofs.yml` run 41585851581,
  `update-live.yml` run 41585854741). `v1.1.0-beta.1`, the first beta of
  M10, is published (2026-11-03, at `5d95e5f`; `update-live.yml` run
  41604126246, `release-proofs.yml` run 41605128705; T-446), then
  `v1.1.0-beta.2` (2026-11-04, at `dbd0fac`) and `v1.1.0-beta.3` (2026-11-05,
  at `6d30328`). Until podssh is stable the expensive checks wait (Q88).

The open work, the defects included, is in [TODO/INDEX.md](../TODO/INDEX.md),
and its order in [TODO/PROGRESS.md](../TODO/PROGRESS.md). The milestones are
in [ROADMAP.md](ROADMAP.md).

## Commands

| Command | State |
| --- | --- |
| `podssh ssh` | **Works.** Through the relay or `--direct`, with OpenSSH's options, host keys, agent, key, password and keyboard-interactive authentication, `-W`, `-J`, `-N`, ptys and exit codes. `node://[user@]NAME`: `exit 3` gave 3 through a node on the live relay (T-084, `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test node_live -- --ignored`, 2026-10-09). The resumable layer replaces a lost leg for 10 minutes (T-153); `iroh:TICKET` (T-163, T-165: `--features iroh-test --test iroh_road`, 2026-10-10), `derp:TICKET` (T-379: `--test derp_road`, 4 passed, 2026-11-01) and the race of roads (T-164, `--test road_race -- --ignored`). Spare links after a silent leg (T-305, `--test roam_at_once -- --ignored`: 3 passed; resume 1.0 to 1.1 s since T-663, 3.0 to 3.2 s before; planted, a client with none failed at 35.1 s). Silent-node notice (T-302, `--test silent_notice`: 5 passed; which terminals keep the title stack waits for the operator's terminals). The direct road after each relay host failed (T-363, `--test direct_fallback`: 4 passed). |
| `podssh each HOSTS -- COMMAND` | **Works** (T-183, T-184, 2026-10-12): one command on each host of a comma list or `@GROUP`, `--parallel` (8; 64 at most), `--output-dir`, `--fail-fast`; the exit is the largest status, 255 for a host with no session. `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test each`: 8 passed on Windows 11. `scripts/interop-each.sh` against OpenSSH and Dropbear: 7 checks passed in CI (cad2885); its planted defect waits for T-251. |
| `podssh health [user@]host` | **Works** (T-189, 2026-10-13; T-480, GitHub #88, 2026-11-03): the far host's load, CPUs, memory, swap, uptime, interfaces, disks and processes from one script through `sh -c`, one line each or `--json`; in a container that a cgroup limits, the CPUs and memory of the limit beside the host's. `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test health`: 4 passed; `--lib health::`: 26 passed; plant `t480-host-cpus` caught. `scripts/interop-health.sh` against OpenSSH 10.3p1 and Dropbear: 4 of 4; planted, `MemFree` for `MemAvailable`, 2 checks failed. `cgroup-interop.yml` (run 41560466473): 5 of 5. |
| `podssh containers HOST` | **Works** (T-190, 2026-10-13): the containers of a host, one line each with the `podssh ssh -t` command that enters it; `--tool`, `--exec NAME`. `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test containers` and `containers::`: 7 and 9 passed on Windows 11. `scripts/interop-containers.sh` (stand-ins of docker and kubectl behind OpenSSH 10.3p1 and Dropbear): 8 of 8; planted, `containers.bad_name` failed. A real runtime waits for a host that has one. |
| `podssh run [user@]host[:DIR] -- COMMAND` | **Works** (T-192, 2026-10-30): copies the project to `~/.podssh/run/NAME` (or DIR), changed files only, then runs COMMAND on the same connection with its exit status; a failed copy is 255. `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test run --test run_far`: 7 and 1 passed, on Linux. `scripts/interop-run.sh` against OpenSSH waits for T-251. |
| `podssh job SUBCOMMAND HOST` | **Works** (T-193, T-197, 2026-10-30): jobs on a far host in its tmux that outlive the connection: `start`, `list`, `log`, `stop`, and a queue (`submit`, `--slots`, `wait`, `fetch`). `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test job --test job_far --test job_queue`: 3, 2 and 3 passed, on Linux with tmux. `scripts/interop-job.sh` against OpenSSH and Dropbear waits for T-251. |
| `podssh proxy HOST PORT` | **Works.** A byte pipe through the relay; `HTTPS_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY`; failover; one-line errors with sysexits codes. T-175: the pipe of `stdio` and `relay:` returns a reply after the end of input (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test pipe_remote`, 2026-10-10). |

## `podssh ssh`, measured

| Check | Result | Command |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `scripts/interop.sh` in the gate: the static binary against OpenSSH (with and without `PermitTTY`, and with PAM) and Dropbear on 127.0.0.1 | **301 of 301**, each check by its id against `scripts/interop-baseline.txt` (T-199, the build image, 2026-10-11, below); 64 of 64 at the gate run of 2026-10-08, after T-238. Exit statuses, 262144 to 5,000,000 bytes with equal digests, keys, passwords, `BatchMode`, PAM, host keys, `-W`, `-J`, `-N`, `-s sftp`, `-tt` and a local pty (`interop-pty.py`). 2026-10-11, in the build image (`sh scripts/dev.sh run`, a debug build): 237 passed, 0 failed, in 480 s. The step `release` of CI at `bc59415` (run 38076724277) hung for its 45 minutes on a port clash with `scripts/interop-cp.sh`; the sshd is on port 2210 now and each client of the block ends at 60 s. |
| The interop harnesses scored against their baseline (T-199; the step `release` in the build image, 2026-10-11: `sh scripts/dev.sh run -- 'sh /work/scripts/gate.sh release'`, 11 min) | `scripts/interop-baseline.txt` names 322 ids and a floor of passes for each harness. ## `podssh cp`, measured

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `scripts/interop-cp.sh` in the gate's `release` step, over SFTP (T-134, 2026-10-09): the static binary against OpenSSH's `sftp-server` on 127.0.0.1 | All 22 cases passed: 0, 1, 262145 and 5,000,000 bytes both ways with equal digests; over an existing file; two files into a directory; `--jsonl` with the SHA-256; `copy-data` within one server; server to server; exits 66 and 73. A planted subsystem that flips one byte exits 70 and leaves the destination as it was. |
| The copy by exec (T-135, the same script, 2026-10-09): OpenSSH with no `Subsystem` line; the same with a `ForceCommand` that prints a banner before each command; the same with its output through `tr '\r' '\n'`; and Dropbear | 0, 1 and 5,000,000 bytes both ways with equal digests on OpenSSH with no `Subsystem`, with a banner `ForceCommand`, with output through `tr`, and on Dropbear (SFTP). Odd names, within-one-server copies and exits 73, 66 and 64 agree with SFTP; no temporary file stays. The gate's interop: 160 passed, 0 failed. Two planted defects fail (exit 70). |
| `podssh mv` (T-138, `scripts/interop-mv.sh`, sourced by the same script, 2026-10-09): OpenSSH with SFTP, the server that flips a byte, and OpenSSH with no SFTP | All 18 cases passed (the gate's interop: 178 passed, 0 failed). Up and down on each road: the source gone after equal digests; within one server a rename, no byte moved; the flipping server 70 each way and the source stays; a growing source 66; server to server; `--jsonl` says `source_removed`. Planted, a digest that always agrees: the move up through the flipping server loses its source, and that case fails. |
| A copy that goes on after a broken connection (T-136, `scripts/interop-resume.sh` with `scripts/fake-relay.py`, 2026-10-09) | Native: 6 unit tests of the side file. Build image (`gate.sh lint msrv_ssh ssh release`), through stand-in relays that end each session after 1,500,000 bytes: 5,000,000 bytes down and up with equal digests on SFTP and by exec, each over 4 sessions; a copy stopped by SIGINT (130) goes on at an offset; interop 184 passed, 0 failed. The planted defect waits for T-251. |
| New relay sessions before the relay's limits (T-137, 2026-10-09) | Native: the budgets sit under the caps; a session is spent before a request that would pass its budget; a direct connection never is. The cases of `scripts/interop-resume.sh` and `scripts/interop-sftp.sh` (T-139) passed in CI's gate: interop 192 passed, 0 failed, at `9841fa5` (run 37934059413). The planted defect waits for T-251. |
| A source that grows during the copy (T-267, 2026-10-10) | A move of a growing source once ended at its 120 s `--timeout` (exit 75; interop 194 passed, 3 failed, run 37987444048). Each road now stops with 66 at the first byte past the starting size. `--lib cp::digest`, 5 passed; planted, a digest with no bound fails. CI at `5557c62` (run 37992184997): interop 203 passed, 0 failed. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: `--result-file` for `cp`, `mv`, `scp`, `run` and `sftp`, in the schema of `ssh --result-file` (T-486, GitHub #97, 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-cli --lib cp::result`, 5 passed; `--lib run_cmd::`, 7; `--lib sftp::`, 10; `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test result_file`, 5; `--test ssh_result`, 5) | **Works.** One object at the end of each run, also of one refused before it connected, mode 0600, `-` refused (64): `ssh`'s keys with `verb`, `error` and `relay.sessions`, plus `files`, `transferred`, `removed`, `status`, `commands` as the verb needs. Each verb's object passed against the test's server. In the gate, `scripts/interop-result.sh` (`result.cp.*`, `.run`, `.sftp`): the step `release`. Planted `t486-bytes-meaning` caught. |

## `podssh proxy`, measured live

| Check | Result | Command |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OpenSSH 10.3 on Windows, then `podssh proxy`, the relay and `github.com:22` | The authentication step is reached (`Permission denied (publickey)` with a throwaway key). The host key is GitHub's published Ed25519 key `SHA256:+DiY3wvvV6TuJJhbpZisF/zLDA0zPMSvHdkr4UvCOqU`. 2.9 s, with the token mint. |
| The same, through a local CONNECT proxy that allows only port 443 | The proxy saw two `CONNECT tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev:443` (the mint and the session). No DNS on the client. |
| A proxy that refuses the relay's port | `the proxy ... refused CONNECT ...: 403 not on the egress allowlist`, exit 77. |
| OpenSSH on Alpine with the static musl binary, in the container | The same authentication step and host key. |
| HTTP to `example.com:80`, stdin closed before the reply | The full reply, exit 0. |
| An unknown host; a private address | The relay's reason (`does not resolve`, `blocked address range`), exit 69 and 77. |
| A real login: OpenSSH on Windows, then `podssh proxy` and the relay to `railway.new` | The shell and `exit 3` work; `ssh` exits 3. |
| IPv6: `podssh proxy 2001:4860:4860::8888 853` (the `%h %p` form), `[V6] 853` and `[V6]:853` | The relay takes the bare address (`/connect/2001:4860:4860::8888/853`), then closes with 1011; exit 69, with the note on the relay's IPv6 route. `V6:PORT` in one word exits 64 and names the brackets. |
| An idle session: `ServerAliveInterval=60`, remote `sleep 600` | It stays up: 602 s, exit 0. |
| The same with `ServerAliveInterval=0` (the control) | The relay cuts it after 184 s (its idle limit is 180 s); `ssh` exits 255. |
| Failover: `--relay-host "tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev:9,tcp-eu-west-3.ssh.relay.ajam.dev"` to `github.com:22` | The first host times out after 20 s and is named. The second host, from the pool, accepts the token cached for the default host and gives GitHub's banner. |
| The order of the relay hosts (T-220, 2026-10-11): `--relay-failover` and `PODSSH_RELAY_FAILOVER`, `staggered` by default | The next host starts when the one before it has not opened within 2 s, or at once when it failed. Build image: a silent first host costs 2 s with `staggered` and `parallel`, 5 s with `serial`, where it cost 20 s. The live relay hosts' open times (`podssh ping --count 3`): 179 to 791 ms from this host, 102 to 437 ms in the Podman box. `cargo test -p podssh-relay --test failover`: 11 passed |
| `unreleased`: a gate for each relay host, shared by the sessions of one process (T-637, GitHub #135, 2026-11-05: `cargo test -p podssh-relay --lib -- gate open failures`, 33 passed; `--test failover`, 13 passed; the relay crate with each serve feature, 881 passed; plants `t637-wait-in-deadline`, `t637-old-answer-opens`, `t637-drop-keeps-slot` and `t637-each-failure-moves` caught) | **Works** in the tests. Up to 8 attempts at once at a host with no failure in the process; after a failure none for 1 s, then one probe at a time, the wait doubling to 30 s, any answer opening the gate; a mint's `Retry-After` up to 300 s. A held session with a next host goes on to it at once. 64 sessions at once on a paused clock dial the host once (not measured). |
| Liveness: the relay answers WebSocket pings | 3 pongs for 3 pings (`cargo test -p podssh-relay --test live -- --ignored`). |
| A 50 s idle session through `podssh proxy` with `ServerAliveInterval=0` | It stays up under the ping watcher (10 s), exit 0. |
| DNS over HTTPS: `github.com` through each of 1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8, 1.0.0.1 and 8.8.4.4; since T-344 (2026-10-31) also Quad9's 9.9.9.9 and 149.112.112.112, and each in RFC 8484's wire form | Each answers through verified TLS (`cargo test -p podssh-ws --test live_doh -- --ignored`: the six and each in the wire form passed in GitHub Actions, `doh-live.yml`, run 41205878813 at `22d9de6`). T-344: wire answers decode, no cut one is taken (`--lib resolve`, 17 passed; `--test doh_wire`, 8). T-405: through a proxy each resolver is asked by name first, then by address (`--test doh_names`, 2 passed; live at `87645d1`, run 41298470792). T-418: HTTP/2 ends with GOAWAY (`--test doh_h2`: 144 runs at 24 at once, none failed). Each plant failed. |
| `--relay-addr tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev=104.21.39.2` (the relay's real address) | GitHub's banner through the relay. |
| `--relay-addr tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev=1.1.1.1` (a wrong address) | Cloudflare's edge there has a valid certificate for the name and refuses the request (its error 1034). podssh fails over to a pool host, and the session opens. |

Planted, the blocks removed from either matcher: its test failed (`--test dial`, `-p podssh-ts --test derp_proxy`, `-p podssh-cli --test doctor`, Linux). |

Live through a cloud container's agent proxy: "... took the CONNECT (140 ms), then nothing came in 5 s: it carries no SSH", `--direct` 255 after 2 s. Planted, the 200 alone trusted: failed (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --lib doctor::net`, `--test direct_proxy`, Linux) |

Live: doctor 31 ok and a login refused through the relay. The speed through the live relay waits for a host to log in to (T-251) | `cargo test -p podssh-ssh --lib relay_stream` |
| A relay's name pinned to loopback (T-318, 2026-10-30) | A name that `--relay-addr` or `PODSSH_RELAY_ADDR` pins to loopback addresses alone goes with no proxy, as a loopback name does; a pin to another address keeps the proxy. Planted, the pin not read: the test failed | `cargo test -p podssh-ws --test dial_pinned` |

Through the proxy they pin no address now, and `m6_exit` and `faults` clear the machine's proxy variables; `m6_exit` 7 passed without iroh. ## `podssh doctor`, measured

| Where | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Windows 11, debug build, no proxy | 16 ok, 0 FAIL, 0 ????, exit 0, about 4 s. Four relay hosts answer `/health` (`tcp-ssh-relay 2026-10-03-r2`) over TLS 1.3 with verified certificates; each line names the address opened. The forward session to `github.com:22` meets GitHub's published Ed25519 key. The clock is within 2 s. |
| The same, through a local CONNECT proxy that allows only port 443 | 17 ok. The proxy allows the relay and `github.com:443`, and refuses `github.com:22` with `403 not on the egress allowlist`. Each relay line says `opened CONNECT ... through` the proxy. The proxy's log lists the same connections as the report. |
| `--relay-host dead-host.invalid,tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev` | Exit 1, 2 FAIL: DNS over HTTPS (`1.1.1.1 answered that dead-host.invalid does not exist`) and that relay host. The token and the forward session use the second host. |
| The compiled-in roots (T-003), Windows 11, debug build, live | The trust store line names `webpki-roots 1.0.9 of 2026-07-18`, and the line "roots age" says 82 days; 17 ok, 0 FAIL. Older than 12 months, the line says so and names `--ca-file` or `SSL_CERT_FILE` (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --lib -- roots_age`). |
| `podssh doctor --json` (T-049, GitHub #9), offline | One JSON object: `schema`, `podssh`, `os`, `arch`, `checks` and `counts`; each text line has an item with the same section, check, status and detail; proxy credentials and a token do not appear (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor -- json`). |
| `podssh doctor --full` (T-052, GitHub #13), Windows, live | The line `login`: through `tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev`, the handshake, GitHub's host key (the one `forward` identified) and the authentication path work, and GitHub refused a key made for the check; 18 ok, 0 FAIL. Offline, one more `????` line and no `FAIL` (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor -- full`). |
| `podssh doctor --full` in the box (T-052), the gate's binary of `d98d77e` | `ok login` through `tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev`; the doctor 29 ok, 0 FAIL; `sandbox-check.sh` 7 ok, 0 FAIL, 1 skip. |
| A Linux host on the tailnet (Ubuntu 22.04), the release workflow's static binary | 25 ok, exit 0, 3.9 s. Open egress; the system trust store added to the compiled-in roots. |
| The `rust:1-alpine` container, as root | 26 ok, exit 0. A copy of podssh runs from `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/root` and `/work`, and is refused in `/dev/shm`, a noexec mount. |
| `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor` | Offline, the network checks are one `????` line, never `ok`. Planted failures (no `HOME`; a proxy setting that cannot be used) give `FAIL` and exit 1. Proxy credentials and a token in the environment do not appear in the output. |
| `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor -- --ignored` | The live path, end to end, exit 0 (3.6 s). |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: The sections at once, `--jobs` and `--quick` (T-511, Q73, 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor_jobs`, 3 passed; `--lib doctor`, 48; `--lib settings`, 9; `--lib mcp::tools`, 6) | **Works.** Through a stand-in proxy that holds the CONNECT of four names in four sections, 3 s each: at once, the four came within 5 ms and the run took 4.8 s; with `--jobs 1`, 16.8 s. `--quick` asked the DERP server nothing and its last line names what it left out. A run of `doctor` on this host: 22.0 s (`--jobs 1`), 18.0 s, `--quick` 18.2 s. Planted `t511-quick-derp` caught. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: The diagnosis of the TLS handshakes, and a reset told from a timeout (T-487, GitHub #99, 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor_tls`, 3 passed; `--lib doctor::`, 53; `--test doctor`, 13; `cargo test -p podssh-ws --lib cut`, 4) | **Works.** Through a stand-in proxy that plays three egresses: names reset after the ClientHello gives one line `diagnosis:` with `tls-names` (`--json`, github.com as evidence); each reset `tls-reset`; each ClientHello unanswered `tls-dropped`: each passed. A reset, a close with nothing and silence are kept apart; Windows' os error 10054 is a reset, in CI's job `windows`. Planted `t487-reset-as-timeout` caught. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: WARP's stand-in echo no more stalls at random (T-599, the gate at `84864f2`; 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-warp --features stand-in`, 71 passed in the lib, 5 in `road`, 3 in the rest) | **Works.** Two causes: a driver that slept with packets left while the queue out was full, and smoltcp 0.14.0's LAST-ACK holding a far end's bytes behind a closed window; CLOSE-WAIT now sends its FIN only once its bytes are acknowledged. A far end closing first with 384 KiB held back sends each byte within 2 s; planted (FIN at once): 3.5 s, caught. The echo test of 1 MiB, 12 loops of 200 at once on 4 CPUs: 0 of 2,400 failed (2 of 2,400 before). |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: An ECDSA key of P-256 whose scalar OpenSSH writes in 31 bytes (T-609, the gate at `b02d8c4`, run 41647533798; 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-ssh --lib key_file`, 10 passed) | **Works.** ssh-key 0.7.0-rc.11 refused each ECDSA scalar under 32 bytes, so one P-256 key of 512 from `ssh-keygen` could not be used, and the gate's interop test failed at random. `vendor/ssh-key` carries one patch (any length up to the field's) through `[patch.crates-io]`; OpenSSH 9.6p1's 243rd key had a 31-byte scalar, and `podssh keygen -y` printed its public key. Planted, the release's decoder: caught. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: The diagnosis counts the resolvers of DNS over HTTPS and n0's relays (T-604; 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-ws --lib resolve`, 19 passed; `cargo test -p podssh-cli --lib doctor::`, 54; `--test doctor_tls`, 3) | **Works.** A resolver asked at its address keeps how its connection ended: against a loopback stand-in, a reset of the ClientHello is a reset over HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2, a close a close, silence a timeout, a dead port a refusal; n0's `/ping` the same. A resolver counts only beside a reference that went with no proxy. Planted, the cut dropped: caught. |
| The token line, Windows, after T-057 | A cache entry written before T-057 names no minting host and is not used: the first run says `minted at tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev and cached`, the next `a cached token minted at tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev (not shown)`. An entry that an earlier failover run had filed under `dead-host.invalid` (GitHub #3) is never used. |

gate's static x86_64 binary of `4bf0c26` (CI run 37783671123), 2026-10-08:

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |

| The probe, planted: the same image with an open network and no filter (`aa9cfaa`) | All 9 required checks of that version fail, exit 1. |
| The probe, planted: the same box with no `/dev/tty` bound | `DIFFERS /dev/tty`: `can't open '/dev/tty': No such device or address`, exit 1. |
| `podssh doctor` in the box | 27 ok, 0 FAIL, 0 ????. No passwd entry, no pty, `bind` refused, `/dev/shm` noexec, DNS refused. The four relay hosts are reached through the proxy; a token is minted; `github.com:22` meets GitHub's Ed25519 key. |
| `podssh proxy github.com 22` | GitHub's banner, exit 0. |
| `podssh keygen`, then `podssh ssh` and `ssh -tt` to `github.com` with that key | Exit 255, `Permission denied (publickey)`: the handshake and the host-key check pass through the proxy and the relay. |
| A prompt with nobody to answer it, on the `/dev/tty` that never answers (T-005, GitHub #15) | `podssh keygen` with no `-N` and no `SSH_ASKPASS`: exit 1 after 0 s; it names `-N ''` and writes no key. `podssh ssh -o StrictHostKeyChecking=ask` to `github.com` with no `known_hosts`: exit 255 after 1 s, with GitHub's fingerprint and `accept-new`. The run exits 0. |
| The same, planted: the gate's binary of `d0b16a3`, before `eacd94e` | `keygen` waits until `timeout` stops it at 90 s (exit 143); `ssh` waits until its 60 s handshake limit. Both steps print `FAIL`, and the run exits 1. |
| The `ssh` of OpenSSH with podssh as its `ProxyCommand` | `No user exists for uid 0`, exit 255: the client of OpenSSH cannot run when the user database has no entry. `scripts/sandbox-check.sh` prints `skip` for it, with that reason. |
| The verdicts of `scripts/sandbox-check.sh` (T-006) | 7 ok, 0 FAIL, 1 skip, exit 0. Planted: the same binary behind a wrapper whose `doctor` exits 3 gives `FAIL doctor`, and the box exits 1. A binary that does not run (`/bin/false`) stops the script with exit 1 before any step. |
| An interactive session over `-tt` from the box (T-004; `BOX_RUN=tt sh scripts/test_in_box.sh`), the gate's binary of `2c39778`, 2026-10-09 | Through a pipe to OpenSSH next to the box, with `--direct` through the box's proxy: exit 7, `vi` saved a file, `less` and `top` drew, Ctrl-C stopped `sleep 30` (20 s in all). railway.new refuses an anonymous visitor (exit 13), so it was not the server. |
| The box from the pinned images (T-206; `alpine:3.20` and `python:3.12-alpine` by the digests of `.github/images/`), the gate's binary of `b082b83`, 2026-10-09 | Each property of the probe matches; `podssh doctor --full` 30 ok, 0 FAIL; `scripts/sandbox-check.sh` 7 ok, 0 FAIL, 1 skip; exit 0. |

## The reverse road, measured

M4's exit (T-085), 2026-11-13: `sh scripts/reverse-in-boxes.sh target/x86_64-unknown-linux-musl/release/podssh`
(`BOX_RUN=reverse` of `scripts/test_in_box.sh`; the binary of `scripts/box/build-podssh.sh`), exit 0. Two
boxes like the target sandbox, on one network, each with the profile `sandbox-a` and the box's CONNECT
proxy as its only way out. The node's box makes the pair (`podssh relay pair m4 --operator-file`) on the
live relay and serves `example.com:80` (`podssh node m4 example.com:80`); the operator's part goes to the
other box through a directory of the host. That box starts two `podssh operator --pair-file ... m4` at the
same second, each sending one HTTP request and holding its input 4 s: both exit 0 (a clean end: the relay's
close 1000), both bodies whole and alike (SHA-256 `8dade617...`), the two sessions from second 1794604214 to
1794604219 each; then `podssh relay revoke m4` in the node's box stops the pair (`m4: stopped; its node was
online`), and the node ends with "the pair was stopped on the relay". The plant `t085-one-after-the-other`
(the second operator after the first): `FAIL the two sessions did not overlap`, exit 1. The run from the
operator's real sandbox follows a release, as the operator's action; the blocking facade's test of two
clients is `pod-relay`'s (Q103).

names an agent proxy on the loopback, whose CA is in `SSL_CERT_FILE` and in
the system's store. The binary: `target/debug/podssh` built there at
`9b6dda2`.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `podssh doctor` | 31 ok, 0 FAIL, 0 ????, exit 0: CONNECT by name through the proxy; the relay's four hosts with TLS 1.3, verified; a token minted; github.com:22 through the relay gives GitHub's ED25519 key. With `SSL_CERT_FILE` unset: 32 ok (121 compiled-in roots and the system's 122) |
| The certificates through the proxy (`openssl s_client -proxy`) | The relay: its own chain (Google Trust Services WE1); github.com: "CCR agent-proxy interception CA (production)". Direct TLS on port 443: "Egress Gateway SDS Issuing CA (production)" for the relay |

| The native tests at `9b6dda2` | 2028 passed, 20 failed, 55 ignored, 22 min 43 s; each failure was of the test or of the place: 16 took the machine's proxy (T-313), 4 dialled past it into the egress (T-315); and one test waited for groff for ever (T-314) |

## Throughput, measured

The method of T-157 (`crates/podssh-cli/tests/throughput_live.rs`): in each
cell, 5 runs, each a new `podssh ssh` and a new connection, of 20 MiB up and
then 20 MiB down, in separate sessions, 300 s at most each. The far end is an
SSH server in the test that drains or sends the bytes; the clock starts when
the far end is ready and stops at the last byte, so the setup of the
connection is not counted. With 5 runs, the p95 is the maximum (nearest
rank).

On the loopback, with no network (a Claude Code cloud container, Linux
x86_64, the debug build, 2026-10-31; `cargo test -p podssh-cli --features
iroh-test --test throughput_live throughput_on_the_loopback -- --ignored
--nocapture`), in MiB/s, min / p50 / p95 / max:

| Cell | Up | Down |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--direct` to the test's server: podssh's own limit | 91.1 / 109.9 / 139.4 / 139.4 | 70.1 / 76.7 / 80.1 / 80.1 |
| The forward road through `scripts/fake-relay.py` (Python's asyncio): the stand-in sets this figure | 4.4 / 4.7 / 4.7 / 4.7 | 5.0 / 5.1 / 5.3 / 5.3 |
| The iroh road through iroh's relay server, UDP off at both ends: each run said that the relay carried each byte | 4.1 / 4.2 / 4.3 / 4.3 | 2.6 / 2.8 / 2.9 / 2.9 |

The iroh row of 2026-10-10 (Windows 11: 34.6 MiB/s up, 25.6 down, at the
p50) was most likely a direct path's: both ends ran with UDP on, on one
host, and with UDP on the cell's runs here say that a direct path carried
them (T-335).

From the same container, through its egress proxy, at 20 MiB a run
(2026-10-31; `PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_IROH_RELAY=https://use1-1.relay.n0.iroh.link.
cargo test -p podssh-cli --features iroh-test --test throughput_live --
--ignored --nocapture throughput_on_each_road`, 442 s), in MiB/s, min / p50
/ p95 / max:

| Cell | Up | Down |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The reverse road through the live relay, with the layer | 2.29 / 2.37 / 2.50 / 2.50 | 1.32 / 1.37 / 1.40 / 1.40 |
| The iroh road through n0's relay `use1-1`, UDP off: each run said that the relay carried each byte | 1.08 / 1.08 / 1.08 / 1.08 | 1.10 / 1.10 / 1.10 / 1.10 |

The iroh row is the same in each run; inferred, it is n0's limit for each
connection (Read: <https://docs.iroh.computer/relays/rate-limiting>).

The forward road through the live relay's own loop (the operator's decision
of 2026-10-31; T-251): `podssh ssh node://` to a node of the cell's own, each
of its connections to the relay carried by a CONNECT proxy of the test whose
upstream is `podssh proxy RELAY 443`, a forward session to the relay's own
host, so each byte crosses the relay twice and the figure is a lower bound;
a run that did not go through that proxy fails. The test's proxy first
carried the bytes with `std::io::copy`, and the forward session got none of
the client's ("1011 target closed before sending anything"); a read and a
write carry them.

The run of T-251 from the same container, at 100 MiB a run (2026-11-01;
`PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_MIB=100 PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_IROH_RELAY=https://use1-1.relay.n0.iroh.link.
cargo test -p podssh-cli --features iroh-test --test throughput_live --
--ignored --nocapture throughput_on_each_road`, 3,087 s, each run passed),
in MiB/s, min / p50 / p95 / max:

| Cell | Up | Down |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `--direct` on the loopback: podssh's own limit | 85.7 / 93.1 / 108.6 / 108.6 | 60.3 / 65.5 / 73.1 / 73.1 |
| The forward road through `scripts/fake-relay.py` on the loopback | 4.24 / 4.54 / 4.77 / 4.77 | 4.54 / 4.76 / 4.82 / 4.82 |
| The iroh road through iroh's relay server on the loopback, UDP off | 3.73 / 3.90 / 4.01 / 4.01 | 2.33 / 2.37 / 2.48 / 2.48 |
| The reverse road through the live relay, with the layer | 1.89 / 2.06 / 2.09 / 2.09 | 1.11 / 1.20 / 1.28 / 1.28 |
| The forward road through the live relay's own loop, to a node (each byte crosses the relay twice) | 1.97 / 2.00 / 2.02 / 2.02 | 0.88 / 1.02 / 1.04 / 1.04 |
| The iroh road through n0's relay `use1-1`, UDP off | 0.97 / 0.97 / 0.97 / 0.97 | 0.97 / 0.97 / 0.97 / 0.97 |

Through the loop the bytes cross the relay twice and still come near the
reverse road's once, so this host's egress, not the relay, likely bounds
both (INFERRED). n0's row is flat, its limit for each connection, as at 20
MiB. A release build that the release check started by mistake ran for 6
minutes of the n0 cell, whose figure its limit sets.

Not measured yet: the forward road to a public server, but through the
relay's loop above, and the direct road (`PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_SSH` names no
server here), and each cell in a sandbox
(the live run of T-251, with 500 MiB a direction in each cell,
`PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_MIB=100`, as the operator ruled on 2026-10-10: Q38).

SFTP by the requests in flight and their size (T-140; Windows 11, the debug
build, 2026-10-12; `PODSSH_THROUGHPUT_MIB=10 cargo test -p podssh-cli --test
throughput_live -- --ignored --nocapture sftp_throughput`): one `podssh
sftp` puts 10 MiB, another gets them back, each with `-R` and `-B`; the rate
is the one that `-v` says, from the first request to the last reply, before
the digest. The far end is Git for Windows' `sftp-server` behind the test's
SSH server. "In flight" is what the window of 512 KiB lets through. In MiB/s,
put / get, one run each:

| Requests x bytes (in flight) | Loopback, `--direct` | The stand-in relay, 50 ms each way |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 x 32768 (1) | 2.01 / 2.00 | 0.22 / 0.22 |
| 4 x 32768 (4) | 5.43 / 7.75 | 0.88 / 0.82 |
| 16 x 32768 (16) | 5.75 / 27.96 | 3.39 / 1.84 |
| 64 x 32768 (16) | 6.57 / 29.39 | 3.40 / 1.85 |
| 1 x 261120 (1) | 7.29 / 14.94 | 1.38 / 1.37 |
| 2 x 261120 (2), the default with OpenSSH | 10.20 / 27.35 | 2.74 / 1.77 |

Through a round trip of 100 ms the requests in flight multiply the rate,
until the window of 512 KiB bounds a download near 1.8 MiB/s. Since T-296
`sftp` names a node, and the same test has a third cell: the reverse road
through the live relay, a pair and `podssh node` in front of the test's
server. Its figures (T-251, 2026-11-01, this cloud container, 20 MiB a copy;
`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test throughput_live -- --ignored --nocapture
sftp_throughput`), put / get in MiB/s by the pipeline: 1 x 32768, 0.24 /
0.25; 4 x 32768, 0.67 / 0.83; 16 x 32768, 1.66 / 1.62; 64 x 32768 (16),
1.40 / 1.50; 1 x 261120, 0.91 / 0.94; 2 x 261120, OpenSSH's default, 1.43 /
1.55. The same run's stand-in relay with 50 ms each way: 0.27 / 0.27 to 3.91
/ 1.87, as above. Through the relay the pipeline matters as much as through
the stand-in, and 16 requests in flight reach the reverse road's own rate
(1 to 2 MiB/s here).

`cp --parallel K` (T-141; the same machine and build, 2026-10-12; `cargo test
-p podssh-cli --test throughput_live -- --ignored --nocapture
cp_throughput`): a copy up and a copy down of 24 MiB, three chunks of 8 MiB,
K at once, each over an SFTP session of its own; in MiB/s, up / down, one run
each:

| Tasks | Loopback, `--direct` | The stand-in relay, 50 ms each way |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 8.72 / 27.49 | 2.76 / 1.84 |
| 2 | 8.80 / 41.10 | 3.68 / 2.27 |
| 4 | 8.78 / 52.09 | 3.69 / 2.85 |

Through the stand-in the tasks share 1.5 MiB in flight, three windows; a
copy up on the loopback is bound by the far `sftp-server`'s writes.

Several files with `cp --parallel K` (T-297; 2026-10-13, `cargo test -p
podssh-cli --test cp_parallel -- small`: 6 passed on Windows 11), against the
test's server and its `sftp-server`: 50 small files go up and come back down
8 at once, with at most 8 SFTP sessions at once on the connection, which the
server counts, and the report lists them in the order of the sources; a
file of two chunks among them goes after them, in chunks; a connection cut
after 1,500,000 bytes goes on over the next one, each file in flight where
it was; a run that ends at the cut goes on in the next run from each side
file; the first failure in the order of the sources sets the code (73,
though a 66 came first); the files of a tree with `-r` go 8 at once both
ways. Planted, one temporary name for every file (`temp_name`; 2026-11-01,
Linux): each of the six failed. The rate of
many small files (T-251, 2026-11-01; `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test
throughput_live -- --ignored --nocapture cp_small_files_by_tasks`): `cp -r`
of 200 files of 16 KiB up and back down, in files a second by the wall
clock: through the stand-in relay with 50 ms each way, 0.7 up and 0.7 down
with one task, 4.6 and 3.2 with 8; through the live relay to a node, with
one task the 200 did not go up within `cp`'s 300 s (exit 75), with 8, 4.6
and 1.6. Some 14 round trips go to each file. Since T-417 (2026-11-01) the
far digest's command starts beside the sync and the close of each file,
and a tree of 50 files or more that go one at a time says once that
`--parallel 8` takes 8 at once (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test cp_tree`:
7 passed; planted, a threshold no tree reaches: failed); measured again,
0.8 up and 0.7 down with one task through the stand-in, 5.3 and 3.4 with
8; to a node, 4.7 and 1.5 with 8. Since T-474 (2026-11-03) the files go 4
at once with no `--parallel`, and that line is gone (its row above).

`cp --delta` (T-142; the build image, 2026-10-12; `scripts/interop.sh`, the
checks `cp.delta.up` and `cp.delta.down`, against OpenSSH and BusyBox's `dd`
and `sha256sum`): a file of 20 MiB with 1 MiB changed went up with 1048576
bytes sent, and the server's copy, with another 1 MiB changed, came down
with 1048576 bytes fetched; the digests agree each way, and no full copy
followed. Planted, blocks compared by their presence only: no block went,
the digests then differed, a full copy followed, and both checks failed.

`cp -r` (T-143; Windows 11, 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test
cp_tree`, 3 passed; the build image, `scripts/interop.sh`, `cp.tree.*`
against OpenSSH): a tree with levels, an empty directory, names with a
space, a link and a FIFO goes up and comes down with the lists of digests
equal and the link a link; the FIFO is skipped with a note and an exit
that is not zero; `--dry-run` says each action and changes nothing;
`--exclude` leaves out a name anywhere and a path from the root; `mv -r`
leaves the tree there and removes the source. A server whose listings name
`../escape` (the test's server, for a user `evil-...`) is refused, nothing
is written outside the target, and the rest of the tree comes. Planted, no
check of the names: the file that `../escape` names is written outside the
target, and the test fails.

`cp -r --delete` (T-144; Windows 11, 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p podssh-cli
--test cp_tree -- delete`, 3 passed; the build image, `cp.delete.*` against
OpenSSH): what the target has and the source lacks goes, a directory after
what it holds; an excluded name stays, a link goes and the file that it
named stays; a dry run lists each `delete` and changes nothing; down, a
file here that the server's tree lacks goes. A copy that fails (a FIFO on
Linux, a file held with no sharing on Windows) deletes nothing, and stderr
says so. `.` as the source of a copy to the login directory is refused
(64). Planted, deletes after a failed copy: that test fails.

Progress, `-l` and an interruption of a copy (T-145; Windows 11,
2026-10-12, `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test cp_progress`, 3 passed and
the test of SIGINT on Unix; the build image, `cp.limit` and `cp.interrupt`
against OpenSSH): the line says the bytes, the percent, the rate and the
time left, and goes to stderr only; `-l 8000` held 1,500,000 bytes to 1.4 s
or more here and 5,000,000 to 4.5 s or more in the gate; `--jsonl` gives
`progress` objects; SIGINT after 2 s gave 130, kept and named the
temporary file and its record, and the same command went on from them.
Planted, the line on stdout: the test of the pipe fails.

What a copy keeps (T-146; Windows 11, 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p
podssh-cli --test cp_meta`, 7 passed, against the `sftp-server` of Git for
Windows; the build image as root, `cp_meta`'s Unix branches, 7 passed, and `scripts/interop-keep.sh` against OpenSSH: `stat -c '%a %X %Y'` equal after `-p`, a sparse file of 100 MiB with 1 MiB of data in 1024 KiB on each side, two hard-linked names one inode): `-p` keeps the access and the
modification times of a file up and down, and of each directory of a
tree, by `cp`, `scp`, `sftp -p`, `get -p` and `put -p`; `--sparse` leaves
holes and each byte arrives; two hard-linked names arrive as one file;
`--owner` keeps the owner, or says why not. That server's rename and stat
set a file's access time: after the rename the times are set again where
they differ. Planted, a local file's modification time dropped: the three
tests of `-p` fail.

`--inplace` (T-147; Windows 11, 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p podssh-cli
--test cp_inplace`, 2 passed, the test of a link on Unix, and `--test
cp_args`, its refusals before anything connects): a second hard-linked name
of the destination saw the new bytes up and down, over a longer old file,
with `--delta` and with `mv`, and without `--inplace` kept the old ones;
the notice came first. The inode check by `stat`, the copy by exec and a
flipped byte that leaves the destination damaged (`scripts/interop-inplace.sh`)
and the planted defect wait for T-251.

`podssh edit` (T-149; Windows 11, 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p podssh-cli
--test edit_args`, 5 passed, and the unit tests of `edit::`,
6 passed): the operand, the editor and the terminal are refused before
anything is attempted (64, 78 naming `EDITOR`, 64), `VISUAL` wins over
`EDITOR` and `vi` on `PATH` comes last, the copy keeps the far name or its
extension, and the far mode is read from a line of `ls -lnid`. The cases
of `scripts/interop-edit.sh` against OpenSSH (an `EDITOR` that adds a
line, over SFTP and by exec, with the far mode kept; no change; a far file
that `podssh ssh` changed first, refused with 73 and the copy named; an
editor that fails; a link) and the planted defect, the check before the
send skipped, wait for T-251.

ZMODEM in a terminal session (T-148; 2026-10-12, `cargo test -p
podssh-ssh --test zmodem`, 20 passed): `podssh ssh` with a pty and
a local terminal finds the start of lrzsz's `sz` and `rz` in the output
(ZRQINIT and ZRINIT, with their CRC), asks on the terminal, receives into
a temporary name with the CRC of each frame and the length checked, then
renames, and sends a local file to `rz` (also by `~Z`, which types `rz`).
The tests check the headers and the subpackets against vectors computed
from the spec's rules, the search for a start, each wait of 10 s with 4
tries, a file each way between podssh's two sides and through the
session's question, a refusal, a name that is taken, and Ctrl-C. With no
terminal there is no watcher. Not measured: against lrzsz itself, as
Alpine has no lrzsz package now; the gate's `pty.zmodem.*` say `env`
without one, and the planted defect waits for T-251.

A passphrase key from `SSH_ASKPASS` decrypts with OpenSSH, a wrong one is refused. An overwrite is refused (exit 1); `-N 'secret words'` is refused (64, nothing written). |

| Check | Result |

Each `-o` keyword is applied, ignored or refused as the parser does it, with the default that `resolve` uses; variables and exit codes match the source; the parser accepts each example; each note names only real flags, keywords and variables. A planted page without one flag fails 6 tests. |

The bytes are the same with an empty environment and with a token, proxy credentials and other settings set; none of them is shown. With no terminal, `PAGER` is not started. |

| `podssh man --json` (T-050, GitHub #10), offline | One object with the options, the commands (availability, arguments, flags with kind and `instead`), the `-o` keywords, the variables, the files and the exit codes; each flag of each working command's `--help` is in it with the same value name, kind and replacement; the bytes are the same with an empty environment (`cargo test -p podssh-cli --test man_json`). |

| groff 1.23.0 and mandoc, Alpine 3.22 | The former `Fl` macro (`\$*`) printed blank flag names in both. The new page shows each flag in both. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: What the report of 2026-11-02 found by trial, in the help and the manual (T-512, 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-cli --lib man`, `--lib help`, `--test man_json`, `--test man_page`; `cargo test -p podssh-relay --test identity_file`) | The notes of `ssh` give the version line `SSH-2.0-podssh_VERSION`; `cp --help` says a broken copy goes on from its side file; `job` says an ID is 8 hex digits; a link as a source says to name a regular file; `proxy` gives `podssh pipe - tcp:%h:%p` as OpenSSH's `ProxyCommand`; the row of 78 names the settings and `ssh_config` files; `keygen -l` takes the private file or its `.pub`. Tests passed: `--lib man` 39, `--lib help` 12, `man_page` 10, `man_json` 4, `man_flag_parity` 6. |
| `v1.1.0-beta.2`: The public services that podssh tries last, and the ones it never names, in the manual (T-533, M10, 2026-11-03: `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test man_page`, `--lib man`) | The notes of `ssh` name the public services tried last when the user named none (the four forwarders, wisp last; tailcat's DERP servers, Least Authority's transit relay, ppng.io, n0's pkarr server), each a third party whose operator sees the target or the ends; the ones never named; and that podssh never uses a domain that looks taken over (Q79). `man_page` 11 and `--lib man` 40 passed; plant `t533-taken-over-unsaid` caught. |
| `scripts/interop-man.sh` in the container gate, the static binary | 68 flag spellings from the `--help` of `ssh`, `proxy`, `node`, `operator`, `man`, `relay`, `status`, `doctor` and `keygen` (gate run of 2026-10-09, after T-082): groff and mandoc show each one. groff `-ww` gives no warning; `mandoc -Tlint` gives no error. The planted page fails (2 spellings missing). |

## Faults between podssh and the relay, measured

`scripts/interop-faults.sh` in the gate, 2026-10-08, **14 of 14** (after T-236). A
stand-in relay and a stand-in proxy fail in one way each, with OpenSSH
behind them.

| Fault | What podssh did | Command |
| --- | --- | --- |
| None (the control) | The stand-in carried a session. |
| A certificate whose root is in no store that podssh reads, as a proxy's that re-signs TLS (T-340, 2026-10-31) | `podssh ssh`, `proxy` and `doctor` end with rustls's `UnknownIssuer` and what to do: the proxy's CA in `--ca-file FILE` or `SSL_CERT_FILE` (replacing podssh's roots) or `podssh-ca.pem` next to the binary (adding). `--test faults -- --ignored a_relay_whose_issuer`, 1 passed; `-p podssh-ws --lib tls`, 1; `--test hostname_verification`, 4; the gate's `faults.issuer`; the plant failed each of the three. |
| A root in the system's store, on Windows and macOS (T-340, 2026-10-31) | The default trust adds `ROOT` of the user and machine on Windows and the trust settings on macOS after `podssh-ca.pem`; doctor's "system stores" line counts each. `--lib stores`, 3 passed (two plants failed); clippy clean for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` and `aarch64-apple-darwin`. Windows in CI (run 41171716387 at `eee611a`): an unknown root refused, the same server passed with its root in `ROOT` (458 anchors). macOS in CI (runs 41176904479, 41177809365): 0 roots read; a domain that holds a root is not measured (the runner refuses it, "NO (-60005)"). |
| A certificate that has expired or is not valid yet names this host's clock (T-345, 2026-10-31) | Each command, not doctor alone: through the fault harness's relay with a leaf valid on 2020-01-01 alone, `podssh ssh`, `proxy` and `cp` said "invalid peer certificate: Expired; certificates are checked against this host's clock, which reads Sat, 31 Oct 2026 07:47:07 GMT". Planted, the clock's branch removed: failed. The gate's `faults.clock` checks it | `cargo test -p podssh-cli --test faults -- --ignored a_relay_whose_certificate`; `cargo test -p podssh-ws --lib tls` |
| The first relay host is down (connection refused) | Failed over to the next host at once. |
| The first host answers the upgrade with 503 | Failed over at once. |
| The first host completes TLS, then does not answer | Since T-220 (2026-10-11, the build image): the session went through the next host in 2 s with `staggered`, the default, and with `parallel`, and in 5 s with `serial`. Before, after the 20 s limit. |
| The first host accepts TCP and does not start TLS | The same since T-220: 2 s, 2 s and 5 s. Before, after the 20 s limit. |
| Two hosts of one deployment in `parallel`, the first opening 500 ms after the second (T-220, 2026-10-11) | One token minted for both; the first host kept, as the earlier in the list; the second's session got a Close at once. |
| The proxy answers 502 for the first host | Failed over to the next host through the proxy. |
| The proxy answers 502 for each host | Exit 255; the proxy's answer is shown. |
| A Close with 1011 during a 5 MB transfer | `ssh` exits 255 with the relay's reason; 351,575 bytes had arrived. |
| A Close with 1011 during the SSH handshake (T-024, GitHub #17) | `ssh` exits 255, and its first line is `127.0.0.1:2201: the relay lost its connection to 127.0.0.1:2201 (relay close 1011: write failed: fault injection)`, then what may help. The gate's binary of `475aea9`, before T-024, said `the connection closed unexpectedly` first. |
| A Close with 1009 (`session byte cap`) | `proxy` exits 69 with the relay's reason. |
| The relay stops: no frames, no pongs, the connection open | The ping watcher declares it dead at 50 s (38 s after the stop). |
| The relay host stops during a session | `ssh` exits 255 after 5 s and names the relay. |
| The first relay host is down, and the second mints the token (T-057, GitHub #3) | The token is cached for the second host; nothing is filed under the first. The gate's binary of `eaf9822`, before T-057, filed it under the dead host: `1 for the dead host, 0 for relay-a.test`. |
| A server that stalls after the key exchange, with `--direct` (T-236): `scripts/fake-stall.py` drops OpenSSH's bytes after its NEWKEYS | `ssh -o ConnectTimeout=10` exits 255 after 10 s: `127.0.0.1 did not answer the first request to log in within 10 s`. The gate's binary of `02e4e1f`, before T-236, waited until `timeout 90` ended it. |

The shapes of T-203, measured natively on Windows, 2026-10-10 (`cargo test
-p podssh-cli --test faults -- --ignored --test-threads 1`, with the tests'
SSH server; the same checks in `scripts/interop-faults.sh`, with OpenSSH,
wait for T-251):

| Fault | What podssh did |
| --- | --- |
| 2 s each way | `greet` and its exit status came back; with `-o ConnectTimeout=5`, exit 255, `did not finish within 5 s` (the planted control) |
| A jitter of 0 to 1.5 s, each chunk in order | 5,000,000 bytes up and back, unchanged |
| 64 KiB/s | 2,000,000 bytes within 0.9 to 1.5 times the 30.5 s computed |
| The connection to the relay ended after 1,000,000 bytes, with no Close | Exit 255: `the connection between podssh and the relay broke` |
| Each byte held 3 s from the start (`pause`) | `greet` came back after the pause |
| The client's address changes (`--move`): the old tunnel goes silent | The forward road, with no resumable layer, ended with 255 by its ping watcher; a new session left from 127.0.0.2 and worked |

A relay host that stops in the middle of a session of the forward road
(T-269, 2026-10-10, with the control of T-156: 20 MiB up and back, and the
relay host's connections end with RST at 8 MiB): `ssh` exits 255 in about
4 s, in each of 110 runs. Before T-269, 7 of 54 runs never ended, and said
nothing after the login.

The exit of M6 (T-156), measured natively on Windows, 2026-10-10 (`cargo
test -p podssh-cli --features iroh-test --test m6_exit -- --ignored
--test-threads 1`, 11 of 11 in 630 s): 20 MiB up and back through `echo` of
the tests' SSH server, with the fault at 8 MiB; the stand-in relay with the
reverse road (`scripts/fake_reverse.py`), or iroh's relay server under a
name that only the stand-in proxies resolve, with no UDP. The client's
proxy makes each fault; the node's makes only the stopped relay host.

| Fault | The resumable layer, over the reverse road | The iroh road | The forward road (the control) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| None | Each byte back, no loss, in 8 s | Each byte back, no loss, in 2 s | (none) |
| A stopped relay host: each open connection, the client's and the node's, ends with RST | Each byte back in 6 to 10 s; one loss and its resume, or two when the client resumes before the node's loss | Each byte back in 2 s; QUIC went on, with no loss of the layer | Exit 255 in 2 s |
| A new address of the client: its connections go silent, and new ones leave from 127.0.0.2 | Each byte back in 40 s; one loss and its resume | Each byte back in 17 s; no loss of the layer | Exit 255 in 62 s (the write limit of 60 s) |
| A stall of 3 minutes: each byte of the client's connections waits 180 s | Each byte back in 208 s; one loss and its resume | Each byte back in 200 s; one loss of the layer and its resume | Exit 255 in 62 s |

The gate's step `m6` runs the same checks in the build image at each push:
11 of 11 in 692 s at `94e3e6a` (run 38001904475). The live run from the box, through the live relay, comes with the checks of
the release (T-251).

A backlog at the relay (T-062, 2026-10-31, in a cloud container, `cargo
test -p podssh-cli --test faults -- --ignored --test-threads 1 a_backlog
the_window`, 2 of 2 in 16 s; each failed on its plant, a try on the next
host for a `1013` and a window of 4 MiB): with the stand-in relay's mode
`backlog`, a `1013 client receive backlog` in the handshake ends `podssh
ssh` with 255, names the close and what makes it, and is tried on no other
host; at 256 KiB/s from a queue with no bound, the window of 512 KiB keeps
what waits under 2 MiB, and 4 MB come whole. The same checks against
OpenSSH are `faults.backlog` and `faults.window` of
`scripts/interop-faults.sh`: both passed in the gate at `b1f75c9` (run
41158061448).

The lines are `wc -l` of each crate's `src/` and `tests/` (2026-10-09, T-082;
`podssh-relay` after T-151).

| `podssh-ws` | 4.5k / 4.7k | TLS 1.3 through rustls with podssh's own pure-Rust provider; TLS 1.2 (ECDHE with AES-GCM or ChaCha20-Poly1305) for proxies that intercept TLS (T-067, T-330: `CC=/nonexistent cargo test -p podssh-ws --test tls12`, 9 passed against OpenSSL 3.5.7 and 3.0.13; `--test crypto_vectors`, 18 passed); certificate and host-name checks with no bypass; HTTP CONNECT and SOCKS5 proxies (T-070: `--test socks5`, 9 passed; `scripts/interop-socks.sh` passed in CI at `385486f`, run 38111621670, interop 288 passed, 0 failed); pinned addresses, DNS over HTTPS (T-344); WebSocket frame rules (T-063, T-081, T-242: `--test frame_lengths`, 5 passed); a direct dial starts each address 250 ms after the one before (T-265: a silent pinned address then the loopback connected in 0.27 s); `plain-ws` for tests only (T-068). | None known. |
| `podssh-relay` | 6.8k / 4.7k | Relay host lists, the cached pool, failover, tokens, the forward opener; the codecs, pairs, node and operator of the reverse road and the blocking facade for podbox (T-071 to T-082, `cargo test -p podssh-relay --all-features`; the node carried two sessions of 1 MiB each way through the live relay, T-079). The resumable layer (`session/`, T-151 to T-155, T-262, T-270, T-354): 31 records tests; a 4 MiB replay buffer each way, no byte of 32 MiB lost (9 passed); 32 MiB each way through four links cut at random with equal digests (9 passed); a heartbeat each 10 s, a loss after 30 s (3 passed); a session through a node on the live relay, idle for 600 s, still echoed; the move before the relay's limits, 8 MiB each way over links capped at 1 MiB, the move at full speed passed 200 runs. The race between two roads (T-164). The identity of each end (T-087, 16 passed). ## Other TCP protocols, measured

The forms are those of [README.md](../README.md), "Other TCP protocols"
(T-179 and T-182, 2026-10-12): (a) OpenSSH listens and `podssh proxy`
carries its session; (b) `podssh pipe` listens; (c) a client runs podssh as
its transport, and nothing listens. The servers run on 127.0.0.1 behind
OpenSSH in the gate's `scripts/interop-desktop.sh` and
`scripts/interop-usbip.sh`, in CI. A check that waits for the operator's machines says so.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| VNC, form a: `ssh -N -L` to TigerVNC's `Xvnc` (no password, local connections only), with `podssh proxy` through the stand-in relay as its `ProxyCommand` | `RFB 003.008` at the local port (CI, 3224a88) |
| VNC, form b: `podssh pipe tcp-listen:127.0.0.1:0 ssh:...,127.0.0.1:5921` | `RFB 003.008` at the listener (CI, 3224a88) |
| VNC, form c: the harness's client (`rfb.py`), which runs `podssh pipe stdio ssh:` through the stand-in relay as its transport, as `ssvncviewer exec=` does | `RFB 003.008 1024x768 podssh-desktop`, and the client exited 0 (CI, 3224a88) |
| VNC, form b where only an AF_UNIX bind works: TigerVNC's `vncviewer` at the socket of `podssh pipe unix-listen:`, on an Xvfb | `CConnection: Using RFB protocol version 3.8` (CI, 3224a88) |
| The bytes of a desktop to TigerVNC's viewer on the loopback, counted on the server's side after the first 5 s: 20 s idle, then 20 s of a screen of new noise 4 times a second | Idle, 0 bytes a minute; busy, 23,060,488 bytes a minute; 64 MiB lasts 2.9 minutes busy (CI, cad2885). The first run (CI, 3224a88) counted 56 bytes down and 14 up, as the image had no font and the viewer quit; `font-dejavu` joins the image |
| Telnet: `podssh pipe stdio ssh:` to BusyBox's `telnetd` | The first byte, IAC (255) (CI, 3224a88) |
| Telnet, form b: BusyBox's `telnet` at `podssh pipe tcp-listen:`, and `echo TELNET-$((6*7))` typed to the shell | `TELNET-42` came back (CI, 3224a88) |
| USB/IP, form b (T-182): `usbip list -r 127.0.0.1` of Linux's tools at `podssh pipe tcp-listen:127.0.0.1:3240`, whose other side is a stand-in of `usbipd` behind OpenSSH | The device 1-1 in the list, and the request that the stand-in read (version 0x0111, `OP_REQ_DEVLIST`) (CI, cad2885) |
| USB/IP on two Linux hosts with the modules `usbip-host` and `vhci-hcd` (T-182): `usbip attach` through the recipe of README.md, a read from the device, `usbip port`, `usbip detach` | Waits for the operator's machines |
| Read, not run (2026-10-12) | SSVNC's `ssvncviewer` runs `exec=COMMAND` and speaks RFB on its stdio; Alpine has no package of it, so the harness's client stands in. TigerVNC's `vncviewer` takes a Unix socket's path, and its `-via` runs `/usr/bin/ssh -f -L`, which binds. FreeRDP's `/proxy:` takes an HTTP or SOCKS5 proxy. No RDP server or client ran. |

## The quick tunnel's edge, measured

From GitHub-hosted runners, with the operator's leave (Q43), by the
workflow quick tunnel edge (T-299, 2026-10-30; runs 41071742798 and
41072705691). The details, with the commands, are in
[design.md](design.md), "The edge, measured". A runner's egress is open; the
box's proxy stands for the measured cage's policy.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| The edge's addresses (SRV and A over DNS over HTTPS) | `region1.v2.argotunnel.com` and `region2.v2.argotunnel.com`, port 7844, ten IPv4 addresses each |
| TLS with `h2.cftunnel.com` and cloudflared's three roots, direct, to three addresses | 7844: TLS 1.3, verified, no ALPN, X25519 or P-256 with no ML-KEM, each name and none; issued by "CloudFlare Origin SSL ECC Certificate Authority", valid to 2038-12-09, and not by a root of the system. 443 and 8443: TCP, no tunnel's TLS. 80: no TLS |
| What the edge sends first after TLS | HTTP/2's client preface: the edge is the HTTP/2 client |
| Through the box's proxy | 7844 gets 403; 443 and 8443 get 200 and no tunnel's TLS; with 7844 let through, TLS verified |
| `POST https://api.trycloudflare.com/tunnel` through the box's proxy, with podssh's own User-Agent | 200 in 3.1 s: `success`, `errors`, and `result` with `id`, `name`, `hostname`, `account_tag` and `secret` (no value printed); no 429 |
| cloudflared 2026.10.6 with `--protocol http2`, `HTTPS_PROXY` set | Registered at `msp01` in 5 s; the page through the name at 12 s; nothing through the proxy |
| cloudflared with no `--protocol` | QUIC, registered at `ord07` in 4 s; the page at 7 s; nothing through the proxy; each precheck passed |
| The relay (`podssh relay trace`, then TLS over `podssh proxy`) to 7844 | The VPC road dialed each address; TLS 1.3 verified in 525 to 617 ms, then the edge's client preface |
| Not run | QUIC from a client of podssh's own, a WebSocket through the name, the bytes of a registration (they hold the tunnel's secret: T-300), and a cage |
| A third party's measurements, not podssh's (T-406, read 2026-11-01) | Eight facts of the edge that cfrs's testers measured on 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-01 (among them the API's 429 with a Retry-After of up to 2994 s, the Version Negotiation of 27 bytes, 413 over 512 MiB), each with its issue and entry: [design.md](design.md), "Measured by cfrs's testers" |

whose client's leg is TLS by name with no upgrade. Drafted and merged on

## The relay

`tcp.ssh.relay.ajam.dev` reports version `2026-10-03-r2` and takes an IPv6
address in the forward path, but reached no IPv6 host on 2026-10-08
([relay.md](relay.md), T-253). Its published document equals the pinned copy
byte for byte (SHA-256
`88eb1b0b8571b829daab17614ea2e27966ba5611951ea28db84660fc41cfa5a8`), and
`python scripts/check-relay-spec.py` passes against it. Limits from
`/relays.json`: frames of 262144 bytes, an idle cut at 180 s, 12 h and 64 MiB
for each session. See [relay.md](relay.md).

Since T-342 (2026-10-31) the pool's cache keeps the `limits` of `/relays.json`,
and a session takes frames up to the relay's `max_frame_bytes` (never under
262144, never over 16 MiB) and keeps its budgets under a smaller limit (`cargo
test -p podssh-relay --lib pool`, 6 passed: 32 MiB gives 28 MiB, 128 MiB leaves
60 MiB, 6 h gives 5 h 30 min; `cargo test -p podssh-ws --lib frame`: 524288
bytes taken at the cap, one over 16 MiB refused).
`scripts/check-relay-limits.py` reads the live limits (10000, 180000, 43200000,
67108864, 262144 on 2026-10-31), each that of
`crates/podssh-probe/facts/relay-limits.toml`; CI runs it on each push.

The reverse road has no idle cut (T-061, 2026-10-09, `cargo test -p
podssh-relay --features pair --test reverse_idle_live -- --ignored --nocapture
--test-threads 1`, 12 runs of 240 s): in each of the 4 quiet runs that no drop
disturbed, the session and both sockets were open at 240 s with no payload and
then carried a byte each way (no frame, a Ping every 20 s from one end, a Ping
every 10 s from each end); the relay sent no keepalive and answered each Ping.
But in 6 of the 12 runs the relay's side ended a socket's connection with no
WebSocket Close, at 24 s to 212 s, also in a control with a byte each way every
60 s (the node's socket 5 times, the operator's 3, once both). No proxy was
set. T-255.

The drops are on the relay's side and come in bursts (T-255, 2026-10-09, `cargo
test -p podssh-relay --features pair --test reverse_drops_live -- --ignored
--nocapture`, from this machine and a GitHub Actions runner, 01:31 to 02:05:38
UTC): this machine held 5 sessions over 2.3 hours with no drop; the runner held
6 over 2.3 hours and both sockets of one pair ended at once at 02:01:01Z.
Before, from 00:27 to 01:28, about 9 drops an hour of a session. Forward
sessions beside them had no drop in 1.2 hours; the relay's idle cut ended 22 of
them at 180.3 s to 180.9 s, with Pings every 10 s. T-255 is M10's since Q63
(2026-11-02), in podssh's own relay role.

The relay's checks of a slow receiver do not operate (T-062, 2026-10-31, `cargo
test -p podssh-relay --features pair --test live_backpressure -- --ignored
--nocapture`, twice from a cloud container and once from GitHub Actions, run
41157016178 of `relay-live.yml`): the control carried 8 MiB in 12.1 s to 13.4 s
and ended `1000` at both levels; a client that then read 16 KiB/s got no Close
in 90 s, neither `1013` nor `1011`, while the node had given the relay each of
48 MiB within 15 s: 48.9 MB that the client had not read waited in the relay or
its edge, on both networks. T-336 is M10's since Q63 (2026-11-02), in podssh's
own relay role.

The harness of T-605 (`scripts/relay-measure.py`,
`docs/research/relay-measurement.md`) measures each host through podssh itself,
one run at a time. From a cloud container through its proxy, 2026-11-03
(`python3 -I scripts/relay-measure.py --podssh target/debug/podssh --stages
pool,trace,forward --hosts 4 --rounds 2 --seed 605`, `unreleased`): 22 runs,
each of 4 hosts opened 2 of 2, answered its trace and passed GitHub's key; the
time to open, p50, 335 ms at `tcp`, then 538, 561 and 729 ms; the WebSocket
ping, p50, 44 to 109 ms. The result holds no proxy address, token or
credential; the self-test passes offline and fails on overlapping runs and on
podssh's lines kept as they are (`scripts/plants/t605-proxy-in-lines.plant`,
caught). From GitHub's network (`relay-measure.yml`, run 41687172170 at
`d629249`, all hosts, 2 rounds): 187 runs, each of the 37 hosts opened 2 of 2
and passed GitHub's key; the time to open, p50, from 138 ms
(`tcp-mx-central-1`) and 187 ms (`tcp`) to 988 ms (`ap-south-2`), the ping from
25 to 296 ms. The stage `dest` against a throwaway sshd on the loopback: the
login 45 ms, 72.6 MiB/s down, 158.0 MiB/s up.

## The release check

`scripts/release-check.sh` checks a release from end to end with no human
(T-251): before the tag, `--local` with the gate's static binary of the last
push and a build of this host; after it, `TAG` with the published files,
whose `SHA256SUMS`, its signature and the provenance of each binary it
checks before any binary runs. Each check is a row with its entry, where it
runs, its limit and its result; `--list` prints each row and what makes it
pass. A row ACTIONS is a check that GitHub's runners make (Windows, macOS,
the box, WARP, Cloudflare's edge, the planted defects and others): the
script reads the run that the session names, and starts none.

A session fills this section from a run:

1. Start the runs that the ACTIONS rows name, at the commit under check
   (`sh scripts/release-check.sh --list --local`, or `--list TAG`, gives the
   command of each), and wait for their end.
2. Run the script with each run and a file for its table. After the tag,
   run it from a checkout of the tag, with cosign and gh on the host:

   ```sh
   sh scripts/release-check.sh --local --binary .work/artifact/podssh \
     --actions-run build=RUN --actions-run release=RUN --table .work/release-check.md
   sh scripts/release-check.sh vX.Y.Z --actions-run release=RUN --actions-run proofs=RUN \
     --table .work/release-check.md
   ```

3. Put the first lines of the table file (the source, the commit, the date,
   the command and the SHA-256 of each binary) and its table below, in place
   of the table of the last run of the same source. The run before the tag
   and the run after it each keep their table. The log of each check stays
   in `.work/release-check/`: the numbers of the throughput run are there.

The script exits 0 only when no row failed and each ACTIONS row was read; a
SKIP names the entry or the question of the operator that holds it.

The release check of the local sources at `6ece1f9`, 2026-11-03T04:14:18Z, on Linux x86_64 (`sh scripts/release-check.sh --local --binary PATH --sums PATH --build PATH --commit 6ece1f92ec2264d81f29cf447c5737546046b38b --actions-run NAME=RUN ... --table PATH --logs PATH`, 17 runs, their ids in the table).
The artifact: sha256 `97ce376b67a39f94e3abe85a2143c9c6514227c57546980f30bb58802aee7b0f`. The build of this host: sha256 `f915d9501a99706677df89a42ebcffc15360fcc9e9105824c1fdaa995433fe7d`.

## Measure again

CAUTION: On a machine with less than 32 GB of memory, set
`CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=4` or lower first. See [development.md](development.md).

```sh
cargo test --no-fail-fast                                 # the default members
cargo test -p podssh-ts -p podssh-cli --features podssh-cli/ts
cargo test -p podssh-cli --test proxy_live -- --ignored   # the network: the live relay
cargo test -p podssh-cli --test doctor -- --ignored       # the network: the live relay
cargo test -p podssh-relay --features pair --test reverse_idle_live -- --ignored --nocapture --test-threads 1   # the network: about 25 min
cargo test -p podssh-relay --features pair --test reverse_drops_live -- --ignored --nocapture   # the network: 15 min, or until PODSSH_DROPS_UNTIL
target/debug/podssh doctor                                # this host, its egress, the relay
sh scripts/sandbox-check.sh target/debug/podssh           # the sandbox record
sh scripts/test_in_box.sh path/to/static/podssh           # the Podman box
python scripts/check-repo.py
cargo todo check                                          # the work record in TODO/
python scripts/check-relay-spec.py                        # the live relay
ssh -o ProxyCommand='target/debug/podssh proxy %h %p' -T git@github.com
sh scripts/dev.sh check                                   # the Linux gate and the static binary
sh scripts/release-check.sh --list                        # the release check (T-251): each row
```

## Hosted development

On 2026-11-25, Codex Cloud ran `sh scripts/cloud-setup.sh --native` with an
empty cache, then again with the populated cache. The pinned Rust 1.99.0
toolchain and locked native dependencies passed an offline build.
`sh scripts/session-start.sh` started both checksum-verified watchers and
the warm run reused them. Local Git tests verified fast-forward startup
and preservation of dirty, ahead and diverged checkouts. The Claude hook
was exercised in the Codex host with Claude's hook variables. Its exported
environment worked in a fresh shell.

`sh scripts/qa-bundle.sh target/debug/podssh OUTPUT_DIR` produced a binary
and manual that passed `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS`. It refused an existing
output directory and a candidate that produced no manual. The CLI also
passed a local `podssh pipe stdio exec:/usr/bin/cat` round trip.

The hosted test instructions use an owned temporary directory and scope
the SSH configuration override to the test command. The cache-directory
test rejected the owner of the Codex sandbox root and passed in the
digest-pinned Debian container. Two detach cases saw zombie children on
the host and passed when rerun under `docker-init -sg --`.

On 2026-11-25, the PodRunner follow-up added the repository license to the
QA bundle. `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` verified all three files and detected
a planted license change. A startup fixture repaired a corrupt watcher
and preserved the dirty checkout. The manual Actions procedure now starts
with direct dispatch. The current Codex network refused the workflow
metadata read through `gh workflow view build.yml -R PodUtils/podssh`.

The default Cargo test build produced 405 executable targets. On Codex,
libtest reported 4,252 passes, 8 failures and 87 ignored cases. All eight
failures passed on targeted reruns. The Debian container supplied standard
directory ownership, SSH configuration and `/proc` behavior. The terminal
case passed with the usual short `/tmp` path, and the key generation case
passed with `umask 022`. `cargo test --locked --offline --doc --no-fail-fast`
completed with no doctests. The OpenSSH TOTP interop case reported that it
could not run because `oathtool` was absent.
